Eight familiar worlds.

A small, traceable set to recognize in your stories—not another system to maintain. Every target comes with a sentence, a meaning, and an explanation you can open when you need it.

Public reader snapshot: 10 September 2026 · 4,493 entries / 72 texts. 100 + 46 additional verb entries + 4 question words. Eight verb entries share target records already in the first 100; homographs and affirmative/negative pairs are explicitly separated within those records.

Use this beside your reader.

Choose 3–5 targets that you actually encountered. Read their sentences, consult the breakdown only as needed, then return to reading. Your word SRS can use the meaning and source sentence on the back. No separate sentence deck is required.

Verification is layered: 140 target definitions have direct lexical-sense support; 10 have explicitly limited/variant support. 133 examples are exact corpus fragments, 16 newly written, and 3 adapted. Every example has an editorial word/grammar analysis. No sentence is labeled native-speaker verified.

Romanization uses ā ī ū ē ō, retroflex ṭ ḍ ṇ ḷ, ch for చ, and ṁ for ం. Amber may represent a whole lexical word or a bound stem—not necessarily an ancient root. Green is used only where a grammatical piece is actually analyzed.

A contrast reference · not one giant new-word batch

Listen for the kind
of answer being requested.

Anchor each question to an answer type. Learn one or two in useful scenes first; compare confusing pairs later. “Where?” and “When?” being similar is a reason to distinguish them carefully, not to introduce ten unfamiliar lookalikes at once.

WHOఎవరుevaruExpect a personWho is she?WHOSEఎవరిevariExpect an ownerWhose book is this?WHATఏమిటిēmiṭiExpect an identity / meaningWhat is this?WHAT / WHAT KINDఏంēṁExpect an action or categoryWhat kind of figure is this?WHEREఎక్కడekkaḍaExpect a placeWhere are these tables?WHENఎప్పుడుeppuḍuExpect a timeWhen is your birthday?WHYఎందుకుendukuExpect a reasonWhy didn’t Ravi come?HOWఎలాelāExpect a manner or conditionHow is the coffee?HOW MUCHఎంతentaExpect an amount / degree / priceHow much does this bag cost?HOW MANYఎన్నిenniExpect a number of thingsHow many books?HOW MANY PEOPLEఎంతమందిentamandiExpect a human countHow many people in total?

Quick contrast: కాఫీ ఎలా ఉంది? asks about its quality. An ఎంత price question asks about cost. The same familiar coffee context can distinguish the questions once each is understood.

26 targets · situation, not synonym list

Meet someone. Keep talking.

Introduce yourself, address another person, accept a suggestion, and keep an exchange going.

“My name is Gopi.” → “Tell me your name.” → “All right.”

001 ↗People & conversationreader #4 · ×94

మీ

mī

your — addressing one person respectfully or several people
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Use మీ before a noun belonging to the listener. It serves both respectful singular and ordinary plural address; here someone asks about a family's baby.

Recognize it Before a family word or possession, మీ signals 'your'; listen for long ī.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 8 ↗ · Sda8fa85cf12d

మీ పాప బాగుందా?

mī pāpa bāgundā?

Is your baby well?

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyour · baby · well-is-QUESTION
మీTARGET
మీrespectful/plural possessor
your

Points to the listener as the baby's family member. The same possessive form can address one person respectfully or several people.

పాపIN CONTEXT
పాపbaby/child
baby; little girl

Names the child being asked about. In this family conversation, 'baby' is natural; the word does not specify an exact age.

బాగుందాIN CONTEXT
బాగాwell+ఉందిis+ఆyes/no question
is well?

A contracted combination of బాగా, ఉంది, and question ఆ. The displayed parts explain its formation, not unchanged pieces to concatenate mechanically.

How it fits together

This is a friendly health check: possessive మీ introduces పాప, and the predicate asks whether the baby is well. బాగుందా contains the yes/no ending -ఆ; it is not a 'how' question grammatically. Telugu puts the question-bearing predicate after the person being discussed.

Notice మీ means 'your', not subject 'you'. Use మీరు for the subject pronoun. The question concerns the child, not the listener's own health.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete question from the turn; the preceding answer is omitted. Translation supplied for this fragment, rather than copying the source's freer 'How is your baby?'.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

The supplied Gwynn entry explicitly defines మీ as the possessive adjective 'your', polite singular or plural. This supports the selected sense, not merely the spelling.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=%E0%B0%AE%E0%B1%80&searchhws=yes&matchtype=exact

Evidence label applies to the lexical sense described above. Inflection, sentence translation, and naturalness are separately analyzed; none is certified by a mere dictionary lookup.

003 ↗People & conversationreader #8 · ×56

మీరు

mīru

you — respectfully singular or plural; plural here
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

మీరు can respectfully address one person or address several people. The counting word in this sentence makes the plural reading explicit: two people are being addressed.

Recognize it మీరు is the person or group addressed; మీ directly modifies a following noun.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 5 ↗ · S36cd6cf5ed41

మీరు ఇద్దరు.

mīru iddaru.

There are two of you.

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyou-PLURAL · two-PEOPLE
మీరుTARGET
మీరుlistener group
you, plural here

Addresses the people being counted. Although this pronoun can address one person respectfully, ఇద్దరు establishes that there are two listeners here.

ఇద్దరుIN CONTEXT
ఇద్దరుhuman numeral: two
two people

The special counting form for two people, not two objects. Learn this whole human numeral rather than attaching a regular plural ending.

How it fits together

Imagine counting the people on the other side of a conversation. Telugu can place a pronoun and a numeral together without an overt present-tense 'are'. ఇద్దరు counts people, whereas రెండు normally counts things. Context, not మీరు alone, determines whether the address is singular-respectful or plural.

Notice మీరు does not always mean several people. Conversely, its use for a group does not imply that every member is an elder.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete counting statement. Translation supplied in natural English rather than the source's literal 'You are two'.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Gwynn entry 1 explicitly gives the personal pronoun 'you', polite singular or plural. Entry 2 is an unrelated verb meaning exceed/transgress and is excluded.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=%E0%B0%AE%E0%B1%80%E0%B0%B0%E0%B1%81&searchhws=yes&matchtype=exact

Evidence label applies to the lexical sense described above. Inflection, sentence translation, and naturalness are separately analyzed; none is certified by a mere dictionary lookup.

005 ↗People & conversationreader #10 · ×55

నీ

nī

your — one familiar listener
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

నీ marks something belonging to one familiar listener. In introductions it precedes పేరు, 'name'; use మీ instead when respectful singular or plural address is appropriate.

Recognize it నీ + పేరు is 'your name'; the long ī distinguishes the possessive form.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 8 ↗ · S786e876bbc35

నీ పేరు చెప్పు.

nī pēru cheppu.

Say your name.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyour-FAMILIAR · name · say!
నీTARGET
నీfamiliar singular possessor
your

Modifies పేరు directly: it is the listener's name. This is a possessive form, not the subject pronoun meaning 'you'.

పేరుIN CONTEXT
పేరుname
name

The information to be spoken in an introduction. No overt object suffix appears here; the verb still takes the name as its object.

చెప్పుIN CONTEXT
చెప్పుsay: familiar imperative
say; tell!

Directs one familiar listener to speak. The sentence does not explicitly name the person who should hear the answer.

How it fits together

For an introduction, the possessive phrase నీ పేరు supplies the object and చెప్పు gives the instruction. Telugu does not need an expressed subject in a command: the listener is understood. Both నీ and the unmarked imperative fit familiar singular address; respectful requests use different forms.

Notice నీ does not mean 'to you'; that is నీకు. Avoid using this familiar command as a default with strangers or elders.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact sentence after an earlier instruction to go. Translation supplied without adding the recipient 'her', which appears only in the broader English turn.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

The returned Gwynn entry explicitly identifies నీ as a singular possessive adjective meaning 'your'. Register is explained through the pronoun system and this familiar command.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=%E0%B0%A8%E0%B1%80&searchhws=yes&matchtype=exact

Evidence label applies to the lexical sense described above. Inflection, sentence translation, and naturalness are separately analyzed; none is certified by a mere dictionary lookup.

011 ↗People & conversationreader #22 · ×37

మనం

manaṁ

we — including the listener
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

మనం groups the speaker with the listener or listeners. It is a subject-form 'we', not simply English 'us'; here the shared group contains three people.

Recognize it మనం invites the listener inside 'we'; మేం leaves the listener outside that group.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 5 ↗ · S7a74ca7ca289

మనం ముగ్గురం

manaṁ mugguraṁ

There are three of us, including you.

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderwe-INCLUSIVE · three-PEOPLE-we
మనంTARGET
మనంspeaker + listener group
we, including you

Includes the person being addressed in the count. This listener inclusion is the central contrast with exclusive మేం, not a difference in politeness.

ముగ్గురంIN CONTEXT
ముగ్గురుthree people+అంfirst-person plural predicate
we are three people

The human numeral ముగ్గురు combines with a first-person plural predicate ending. Final ఉ is replaced in the surface form; the pieces are underlying forms.

How it fits together

This is a small-group headcount that includes the listener. మనం identifies the inclusive group, and ముగ్గురం makes 'three people' a first-person plural predicate. English adds 'there are' or 'we are'; Telugu carries the predication in the numeral's ending without a separate present-tense copula.

Notice Do not gloss మనం as an object-case 'us' everywhere. Also do not swap it with మేం when the listener belongs to the group.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete nominal sentence, including the source's lack of final punctuation. Translation supplied with inclusive meaning made explicit.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Gwynn pronoun entry 1 explicitly defines inclusive 'we'. The separate noun homonym 'mind' is irrelevant. The counting ending is grammatical analysis beyond that lexical entry.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=%E0%B0%AE%E0%B0%A8%E0%B0%82&searchhws=yes&matchtype=exact

Evidence label applies to the lexical sense described above. Inflection, sentence translation, and naturalness are separately analyzed; none is certified by a mere dictionary lookup.

013 ↗People & conversationreader #26 · ×33

అవును

avunu

yes — confirming something
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

అవును confirms a question or statement. It can form a complete conversational turn by itself; its function differs from agreeing to carry out a request with సరే.

Recognize it Hear అవును as confirmation: 'yes, that is so'.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 8 ↗ · S42165fd7f56b

అవును.

avunu.

Yes.

Take this sentence apart 1 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyes-CONFIRMATION
అవునుTARGET
అవునుaffirmative response
yes

A whole response confirming the preceding proposition. Do not split it into guessed verb endings merely because it resembles forms of 'become'.

How it fits together

A conversational answer can be complete without repeating the question's subject or predicate. అవును supplies confirmation, while the preceding turn supplies what is being confirmed. This card teaches the response itself, not an invented question. Contrast factual confirmation with సరే, which often accepts a request or proposal.

Notice Brown supports the confirmation sense, not a universal replacement for every English 'okay' or every positive verb answer. A one-word confirmation is a valid complete conversational response.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete one-word response, with no physical speaker label. Translation supplied; no unverified preceding question is invented for the card.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Read Brown అవును (p. 97): the entry body gives అవును or ఔను and explicitly defines 'It is so, yes'. This directly supports the complete confirmation response. Its displayed roman heading 'avutu' appears inconsistent with the Telugu; retain the card's avunu rather than copying that heading. Gwynn's unsuccessful lookup is not evidence against the Brown definition, and Brown's historical verb analysis is not used to claim native-verified segmentation.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/brown_query.py?qs=అవును&searchhws=yes&matchtype=default

Evidence label applies to the lexical sense described above. Inflection, sentence translation, and naturalness are separately analyzed; none is certified by a mere dictionary lookup.

015 ↗People & conversationreader #28 · ×32

నేను

nēnu

I
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

నేను names the speaker as the subject. In this photograph conversation, the speaker says they are absent from the picture, not that they do not exist.

Recognize it నేను identifies the speaker; a matching ending such as -ను can also mark first person on the predicate.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 7 ↗ · Sdcd510c4a435

నేను లేను.

nēnu lēnu.

I am not in it.

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderI · absent-FIRST-SINGULAR
నేనుTARGET
నేనుspeaker as subject
I

Contrasts the speaker with relatives visible in the photograph. The explicit pronoun helps make that contrast clear even though the predicate marks first person.

లేనుIN CONTEXT
లేnegative existence/presence stem+నుfirst-person singular
am not there

The first-person singular negative of presence. It says the speaker is absent from the relevant place or picture, not that an identity description is false.

How it fits together

While looking at a photograph, the speaker contrasts visible family members with themself. నేను is the subject, and లేను supplies negative presence with first-person agreement. The location is recoverable from the preceding sentence and need not be repeated. This differs from using కాదు to deny a noun-based identification.

Notice Do not translate this isolated fragment as a dramatic 'I do not exist'. Its location is the photograph established in the source turn.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete sentence from a turn identifying family members in a photograph. Translation supplied; 'in it' refers to that photograph and is contextually understood.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Gwynn directly defines నేను as 'I, myself'. The first-person negative form లేను and its photograph interpretation are contextual grammar, not additional claims from this pronoun entry.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=%E0%B0%A8%E0%B1%87%E0%B0%A8%E0%B1%81&searchhws=yes&matchtype=exact

Evidence label applies to the lexical sense described above. Inflection, sentence translation, and naturalness are separately analyzed; none is certified by a mere dictionary lookup.

017 ↗People & conversationreader #30 · ×30

ఆయన

āyana

he; that man — respectful reference
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఆయన refers to a man respectfully, not necessarily only an elder. Here it introduces a question about who he is; no relationship such as husband is asserted.

Recognize it ఆయన points respectfully to a man; ఎవరు asks who that man is.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 1 ↗ · S98625b4b90f2

ఆయన ఎవరు?

āyana evaru?

Who is he?

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderhe-RESPECTFUL · WHO
ఆయనTARGET
ఆయనrespectful male reference
he; that man

Refers to the man being identified rather than addressing him as 'you'. Respect does not require the referent to be older than the speaker.

ఎవరుIN CONTEXT
ఎవరుWHO: person's identity
who?

Asks for a person's identity, name, or role. Its shape is not evidence that the question must concern several people.

How it fits together

To identify a man, the pronoun establishes the referent and ఎవరు supplies the missing identity. A present-time copula is unnecessary. The respect conveyed by ఆయన belongs to how the speaker refers to him; the question word remains usable for a single person or multiple people in other contexts.

Notice Do not translate ఆయన automatically as 'husband'. That reading requires an appropriate possessive and conversational context, absent here.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete source question. Translation supplied; English 'he' does not itself reproduce the Telugu respectful register.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Gwynn gives 'he, that man' and separately documents husband in possessive constructions. The selected standalone pronoun sense is supported; social-register guidance is grammatical usage advice.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=%E0%B0%86%E0%B0%AF%E0%B0%A8&searchhws=yes&matchtype=exact

019 ↗People & conversationreader #32 · ×29

ఆమె

āme

she; that woman
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఆమె refers to a woman being discussed or indicated. It is a useful ordinary personal reference; the source identifies her by name rather than using an object demonstrative.

Recognize it ఆమె followed by a name often gives the pattern 'she is [name]'.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 8 ↗ · S0723b4da187e

ఆమె ఉమ.

āme Uma.

She is Uma.

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction
Meaning ordershe · Uma
ఆమెTARGET
ఆమెfemale person reference
she

Refers to the woman being introduced. In the surrounding turn it contrasts with other women referred to more proximally as ఈమె.

ఉమIN CONTEXT
ఉమproper name
Uma, a person's name

The name supplied as the woman's identity. Preserve the source spelling; no family relationship or dictionary meaning needs to be attached to the name.

How it fits together

A pronoun followed by a name can form a complete identification: ఆమె ఉమ. English requires 'is', but Telugu does not require an overt present-tense copula here. The name functions as the predicate, not a speaker label. The source's broader introduction distinguishes this woman from several other named people.

Notice Do not replace ఆమె with ఇది when aiming for ordinary respectful personal reference. Also avoid translating the proper name as a common noun.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete sentence within a longer set of introductions. Translation supplied only for this identification; later family relationships are not folded into it.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Gwynn directly defines ఆమె as 'she, that woman'. That supports the personal pronoun here. ఉమ is retained as the source person's name, not treated as vocabulary for a common noun.

036 ↗People & conversationreader #57 · ×23

బాబూ

bābū

sir!; dear boy! — male address, respectful here
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

బాబూ addresses a male person; tone and relationship can make it respectful or affectionate. Here 'sir' fits the source reply, but humility is not an inherent grammatical meaning.

Recognize it Final long ū in బాబూ often signals calling or addressing someone, rather than mentioning బాబు.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 36 ↗ · Seae6db317acd

అవును బాబూ!

avunu bābū!

Yes, sir!

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyes · male-addressee-VOCATIVE
అవునుIN CONTEXT
అవునుconfirmation
yes

Confirms the preceding statement or question. The added address does not change it into a verb or reveal precisely what earlier proposition was affirmed.

బాబూTARGET
బాబుmale addressee+ఊvocative vowel lengthening
sir!

Final ఉ becomes long ఊ for direct address. The underlying base and vowel-change label are explanatory; the source uses the respectful 'sir' reading here.

How it fits together

The response has two jobs: అవును confirms something and బాబూ addresses the listener. A vocative sits outside the proposition rather than acting as its subject or object. The long final vowel is an address signal. Context determines whether this male address sounds respectful, affectionate, or otherwise socially marked.

Notice Do not infer the listener is a child, the speaker's son, or named Babu. This is not a universally safe replacement for all honorific forms.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete response with the spoken male address retained. Translation supplied using the source's respectful reading, not an invented personal name 'Babu'.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Read Gwynn బాబూ! (p. 380), returned by beginning-with బాబూ: it explicitly describes respectful and friendly address to a male of any age, with 'sir!' and 'young man!' as equivalents. This directly attests the long-vowel address form and selected sense; it is no longer supported only through బాబు. The base-plus-vowel-lengthening display is still grammatical explanation, not an independently reviewed morphological derivation.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=బాబూ&searchhws=yes&matchtype=default

038 ↗People & conversationreader #64 · ×22

మేం

mēṁ

we — excluding the listener
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

మేం refers to the speaker's group without the listener. In this counting exchange, two speakers contrast their group with the listener before counting everyone together with మనం.

Recognize it మేం counts the speaker's side; మనం can include the person being spoken to.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 5 ↗ · S93e89b524b8c

మేం ఇద్దరం.

mēṁ iddaraṁ.

There are two of us, not including you.

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderwe-EXCLUSIVE · two-PEOPLE-we
మేంTARGET
మేంspeaker group excluding listener
we, not including you

The short form corresponding to మేము. Here the speaker's group is counted separately from the listener, so the exclusion is visible in context.

ఇద్దరంIN CONTEXT
ఇద్దరుtwo people+అంfirst-person plural predicate
we are two people

The human numeral ఇద్దరు takes a first-person plural predicate ending, replacing final ఉ. That ending marks 'we' but does not itself encode listener exclusion.

How it fits together

This source turn provides a built-in contrast: the speaker's group has two people, the listener is counted separately, and the combined group is then called మనం. In the selected sentence, మేం carries exclusion while ఇద్దరం predicates a human count with first-person plural marking. No separate present-tense 'are' is needed.

Notice Do not infer inclusive or exclusive meaning from the predicate ending alone. Gwynn explicitly establishes exclusion for మేం/మేము; the source context also contrasts this group with the listener.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete first sentence. The turn next counts the listener separately, then asks about the combined group with మనం. Translation supplied to make this exclusion explicit.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Read Gwynn's combined headword మేం, మేము (p. 437), returned by beginning-with మేం: it explicitly defines 'we' excluding the person or people addressed. Both the short form and listener exclusion are directly supported, resolving the earlier cross-reference. The ending in ఇద్దరం is a separate grammatical analysis; it does not itself encode exclusion.

  • Evidence 1 ↗ https://dsal.uchicago.edu/cgi-bin/app/gwynn_query.py?qs=మేం&searchhws=yes&matchtype=default

Evidence label applies to the lexical sense described above. Inflection, sentence translation, and naturalness are separately analyzed; none is certified by a mere dictionary lookup.

041 ↗People & conversationreader #67 · ×21

నాన్నగారూ

nānnagārū

Father! / Dad! (respectful address here)

Nā is the possessive modifier my before a noun. It differs from nēnu, I, and from nāku, to me; its long vowel matters.

Recognize it Nā plus a noun usually means my plus that noun; nā pēru is a useful complete recognition unit.

My name is Gopi.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning ordermy | name | Gopi
నాTARGET

my

పేరుIN CONTEXT

గోపిIN CONTEXT

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderher.FAMILIAR | name | Sarala
దానిTARGET
దానిadi-family possessive or oblique
her, familiarly

Dāni points back to the daughter introduced with adi. It marks the possessor of the name; the final ni is not an object marker here.

పేరుIN CONTEXT
పేరుname
name

Pēru is the thing possessed, so dāni pēru means her name in this context. No additional possessive linker appears between the words.

సరళIN CONTEXT
సరళpersonal name
Sarala

The personal name supplies the identifying predicate. It should be read as a name here, not translated by an unrelated dictionary adjective sense.

How it fits together

The preceding context establishes a female relative, so dāni is translated her rather than its. Dāni pēru forms the possessive subject phrase and Saraḷa identifies the name. There is no overt copula. The same pronoun family can refer to things, making antecedent and social context essential.

Notice Do not use dāni as a universally neutral equivalent of her. Āme or āviḍa-based reference is safer when respect is required.

అతనితోIN CONTEXT

Atanu takes the oblique atani before tō. The phrase marks the conversation partner; English may naturally translate talk to him rather than talk with him.

జాగ్రత్తగాIN CONTEXT

Jāgratta combines with gā to describe how to speak. It modifies the action, not the person, so carefully is more accurate than a careful man.

మాట్లాడుTARGET
మాట్లాడుspeak: familiar singular command

The whole lexical verb serves as a direct command. Do not manufacture a separate productive suffix from its final lāḍu when explaining this chosen form.

How it fits together

Atanitō identifies the person to speak with, while jāgrattagā specifies the manner. Māṭlāḍu is the familiar singular imperative directed to an understood you. The comitative tō belongs to the conversation partner, not the grammatical subject. The adverbial gā turns care or caution into carefully.

Notice This sentence advises manner, not which language to speak. It does not illustrate the dictionary's negotiate or engage-a-vehicle senses.

ప్రేమించు expresses love, including affection for family, not exclusively romance. For simply liking a food or object, ఇష్టం is often the more suitable construction.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderI | mother.OBJECT | love.PROGRESSIVE.1SG
నేనుIN CONTEXT
నేనుfirst-person singular pronoun
I

This identifies the person experiencing love. It is a nominative subject here, unlike the dative experiencer used in నాకు ఇష్టం.

అమ్మనుIN CONTEXT

Although morphologically progressive, the whole expression can describe ongoing affection. Idiomatic English normally uses love rather than am loving in this context.

How it fits together

The experiencer is the subject నేను, and the loved person అమ్మను receives an accusative suffix. ప్రేమిస్తున్నాను agrees with the first-person singular subject. Notice Do not label the verb romantic-only, or mechanically substitute it whenever English casually says love a snack.

15 targets · situation, not synonym list

Ask for the missing information.

Ask about a person, an object, a place, a time, a reason, or a quality. This is also a contrast reference—not a demand to memorize every question word together.

“Who?” expects a person. “Where?” expects a place. “When?” expects a time.

ఏం

ēṁ

what; what kind of — before a noun here
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఏం asks for unknown content or identity. Here it modifies బొమ్మ to ask what the toy represents; it is not restricted to adjective-like uses everywhere.

Recognize it Prioritize ఏం: it marks the information gap, while the following noun tells you what category to answer with.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 7 ↗ · Sf372402fbd4a

ఇది ఏం బొమ్మ?

idi ēṁ bomma?

What kind of toy is this?

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis · WHAT-KIND-OF · toy
ఇదిIN CONTEXT
ఇదిnearby thing
this

Points to the nearby object whose identity is being asked about. The question meaning comes from ఏం, not from ఇది.

ఏంTARGET
ఏంWHAT: unknown kind
what kind of

The question focus: what sort of toy, or a toy of what? Keep this whole interrogative highlighted rather than inventing suffix boundaries.

బొమ్మIN CONTEXT
బొమ్మtoy or figure
toy; doll/figure here

The object category being identified. In this lesson's toy context, the answer may identify an animal figure rather than a human doll.

How it fits together

When someone points to a toy, ఇది establishes the referent and ఏం బొమ్మ asks its kind or identity. No separate present-tense 'is' appears. Telugu keeps the interrogative within its ordinary noun phrase; it need not move the question word to the beginning as English often does.

Notice ఇది means 'this', never 'which' in this question. ఏం has the dictionary-supported interrogative sense 'what'; 'what kind of' expresses its role with బొమ్మ here, not a restriction on all its uses.

ఎవరు requests a person's identity and can refer to one person or several. This short introduction question asks who a woman is, not which object is hers.

Take this sentence apart 2 words · meaning + construction

ఎవరుTARGET
ఎవరుWHO: identity question

The key information-seeking word. A name, relationship, or role can answer it; a place or time would answer a different question.

ఎన్ని

FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఎన్ని asks for quantity, typically the number of things. Here the things are books; counting people usually calls for a human-count expression such as ఎంతమంది.

Recognize it Prioritize ఎన్ని as HOW MANY: listen for the noun that tells you what is being counted.

Meaning orderthese · book-PLURAL · HOW-MANY
ఈIN CONTEXT
ఈnearby noun modifier
these, before a plural noun

Points to the books within the current group. This demonstrative modifier is unchanged for singular and plural nouns; పుస్తకాలు supplies the plurality.

పుస్తకాలుIN CONTEXT

Requests a number for the identified books. It does not ask their names, owners, or location; those require different question words.

Telugu leaves the quantity question after the established topic rather than requiring English-style question-word fronting.

Notice Do not use an unrestricted English countable/uncountable rule: the dictionary also documents ఎన్ని with Telugu plural-form mass nouns. For people, learn human-count constructions separately.

FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

A confirmation or reminder particle appealing to shared knowledge. In this sentence it reminds someone of an available knife rather than seeking wholly unknown information.

Recognize it At the end of a statement, కదా asks for recognition or agreement; it does not negate the statement.

Meaning orderknife | exists | confirmation.REMINDER
కత్తిIN CONTEXT
కత్తిknife

Names the available utensil. The double త్త represents a geminate consonant and is part of the noun, not a grammatical suffix.

ఉందిIN CONTEXT

Asserts that the knife is present or available. The existence construction introduces the object without requiring a separate English-style there word.

కదాTARGET

ఎవరి asks about possession or association and is the possessive or oblique form of ఎవరు, who. Here it asks who owns the indicated book.

Recognize it ఎవరు → ఎవరి before an owned noun; ఎవరికీ/ఎవరికి add further case or emphasis according to context.

Whose book is this?

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis | whose | book
ఇదిIN CONTEXT
ఇదిnear singular nonmasculine pronoun
this

Refers to the book being indicated. It functions independently as the subject rather than modifying పుస్తకం as the determiner ఈ would.

ఎవరిTARGET

Asks for the person associated with ownership of the book. The final vowel alternation is not a general detachable రి possessive suffix.

పుస్తకంIN CONTEXT

Completes the predicate noun phrase whose book. It remains singular and unmarked, since the sentence identifies ownership rather than describing an action.

Notice ఎవరు asks who; ఎవరి before a noun asks whose. Avoid treating every word ending in రి as a possessive pronoun.

కోసం

kōsaṁ

for; for the sake of
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Kōsaṁ follows the person or purpose something is for. Unlike English for, it is a postposition and therefore comes after its associated noun or pronoun.

Recognize it Look left of kōsaṁ to find the beneficiary or purpose: nā kōsaṁ, for me; evari kōsaṁ, for whom.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 58 ↗ · S79d908e46b23

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderwho.OBLIQUE | for | bring.PAST.YOU
ఎవరిIN CONTEXT
ఎవరిoblique form of evaru
who; whose

This is the evari form of who before a postposition. Although evari alone often means whose, evari kōsaṁ together means for whom.

కోసంTARGET
కోసంbeneficiary or purpose postposition
for

Kōsaṁ follows evari and identifies the intended beneficiary. It is a whole postposition, not a case suffix attached to the preceding token.

తెచ్చావుIN CONTEXT
తెచ్చ్tecc- past stem of bring+ఆవుsecond-person singular past ending
you brought

The past tecc- stem belongs to the bring verb teccu. Āvu marks the familiar singular addressee; the object plates is understood from context.

How it fits together

Evari kōsaṁ is a question phrase asking for the beneficiary, with the postposition after the interrogative. Teccāvu identifies a past action by the person being addressed. Telugu omits both you and the plates because agreement and conversational context make them recoverable. The question word supplies the interrogation.

Notice For whom is the phrase meaning; translating each word separately as whose for would misrepresent the construction.

ఎక్కడ

ekkaḍa

where; at what place
QUESTION PRIORITYLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఎక్కడ asks for location, not time, manner, or amount. Contrast ఎప్పుడు when, ఎలా how, and ఎంత how much; ఎక్కడికి specifically asks where to.

Recognize it ఎక్కడ → place; ఎప్పుడు → time; ఎలా → manner/condition; ఎంత → amount/degree. Destination adds కి: ఎక్కడికి.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 4 ↗ · S1e80fb2f6076

ఈ బల్లలు ఎక్కడ ఉన్నాయి?

Where are these benches?

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthese | benches.PLURAL | where | exist.NONHUMAN-PLURAL
ఈIN CONTEXT
ఈproximal demonstrative
these; this

The demonstrative does not change for plural reference. English uses these because the following noun and existence verb are plural.

బల్లలుIN CONTEXT
బల్లbench/table noun+లుplural suffix

The suffix makes the furniture noun plural. The source translation selects benches; the noun can refer to tables in other settings.

ఎక్కడTARGET
ఎక్కడlocation question word
where

This asks for a place as the answer. It does not request a time, a quality judgment, or a quantity.

ఉన్నాయిIN CONTEXT
ఉన్నexistential base related to ఉండు+ాయిnonhuman plural ending

The plural existence form agrees with the benches. It expresses their location, not an action happening continuously merely because it resembles a progressive auxiliary.

How it fits together

ఈ బల్లలు is a plural nonhuman subject, so the existential predicate is ఉన్నాయి. ఎక్కడ occupies the missing location slot without moving to the sentence's beginning as English where does. Answer with a place: here, there, or in a room. Use ఎప్పుడు for time, ఎలా for manner or condition, and ఎంత for amount instead.

Notice ఎక్కడ is location; ఎక్కడికి adds destination. Do not interchange this question with ఎప్పుడు simply because both seek contextual information.

ఎప్పుడు

eppuḍu

when; at what time
QUESTION PRIORITYLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఎప్పుడు asks for a time, date, or occasion. Contrast ఎక్కడ where, ఎలా how, and ఎంత how much; it does not by itself ask for elapsed duration.

Recognize it ఎప్పుడు → when/date; ఎక్కడ → where; ఎలా → how; ఎంత → how much. Watch the added long ū in ఎప్పుడూ.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 7 ↗ · Sba5fd5de6167

నీ పుట్టినరోజు ఎప్పుడు?

nī puṭṭinarōju eppuḍu?

When is your birthday?

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyour.FAMILIAR | born.RELATIVE-day | when
నీIN CONTEXT
నీsecond-person singular possessive
your; familiar singular

This possessive refers to one familiar listener. It modifies birthday and is not the independent subject pronoun నువ్వు in this sentence.

పుట్టినరోజుIN CONTEXT
పుట్ట్bound stem of పుట్టు, be born+ినpast relative-participle suffix+రోజుday noun; compound component
birthday

The compound literally associates a born form with day. It is a conventional birthday noun, not a separate finite statement saying someone was born.

ఎప్పుడుTARGET
ఎప్పుడుtime question word
when

This requests the date or time associated with the birthday. A place or a description of its quality would answer a different question.

How it fits together

నీ పుట్టినరోజు identifies the event, and ఎప్పుడు requests its date. Telugu can omit an overt present-tense copula in this compact question, whereas English inserts is. Answer with a date or time, not a place for ఎక్కడ, a manner for ఎలా, or an amount for ఎంత. Duration instead requires an expression such as ఎంత సేపు.

Notice ఎప్పుడూ with final long ū often means always or ever in context; it is not identical to the plain question ఎప్పుడు.

ఎలా

elā

how; in what way; in what condition
QUESTION PRIORITYLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఎలా asks about manner or condition, including how something tastes. Contrast ఎక్కడ location, ఎప్పుడు time, and ఎంత quantity or price; quality and cost are different questions.

Recognize it ఎలా → manner/condition; ఎంత → amount/degree. Compare coffee quality with coffee price, while ఎక్కడ and ఎప్పుడు ask place and time.

Meaning ordercoffee | how | be.SINGULAR-NONMASC

This agrees with the singular drink and supports a question about its condition. Keeping the irregular form whole avoids inventing a misleading stem division.

మీద

మీద follows the supporting surface to locate something on it. In this search for a pen, the book is the surface; no abstract 'about' sense is involved.

Recognize it Surface noun + మీద tells you what something is on.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 4 ↗ · Sf7c43a804a61

నీ కలం పుస్తకం మీద ఉందా?

nī kalaṁ pustakaṁ mīda undā?

Is your pen on the book?

Take this sentence apart 5 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyour · pen · book · on · is-QUESTION
నీIN CONTEXT
నీfamiliar singular possessor
your

Identifies the pen as the listener's. It modifies కలం only; the sentence does not explicitly say that the book belongs to the listener.

కలంIN CONTEXT
కలంobject being located
pen

The object whose location is questioned. It remains the subject of ఉంది, while the book supplies the reference surface.

పుస్తకంIN CONTEXT
పుస్తకంsupporting surface

Names the object used as a location reference. The following postposition మీద relates the pen to the book's surface.

మీదTARGET
మీదsurface-location relation
on

Comes after the surface noun, unlike English 'on' before 'the book'. Here it specifies physical placement, not any metaphorical relation.

ఉందాIN CONTEXT
ఉందిis/present+ఆyes/no question

ఉంది combines with question -ఆ and surfaces as ఉందా. The final vowel change creates a yes/no location question rather than a statement.

How it fits together

When looking for a pen, నీ కలం names the item and పుస్తకం మీద proposes its location. Telugu places the spatial relation after its noun, so 'on the book' is literally 'book on'. ఉందా asks whether the proposed location holds; its -ఆ marks a yes/no question, not 'where'.

Notice మీద is not possessive మీ plus a suffix. Treat it as a whole postposition; this example asks 'on the book?', not 'above somewhere near it?'.

తాతయ్య

grandfather — familiar/respectful family term
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

తాతయ్య is a familiar respectful family term for grandfather. Here it identifies the owner of a book; it is not a spoken address to grandfather.

Recognize it తాతయ్య + an object often means 'Grandfather's object'; final long ā instead can mark direct address.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 15 ↗ · Saf96ac213b0f

ఇది తాతయ్య పుస్తకం కాదు.

idi tātayya pustakaṁ kādu.

This is not Grandfather's book.

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis · grandfather · book · is-not
ఇదిIN CONTEXT
ఇదిitem under discussion
this

Refers to the book currently being considered. The demonstrative alone does not indicate who owns it; that proposed relation appears in the following phrase.

తాతయ్యTARGET
తాతgrandfather+అయ్యrespectful/familiar kinship extension
grandfather; grandfather's here

The full kinship word is dictionary-supported; తాత plus అయ్య is an explanatory formation analysis, not a derivation established by that entry. Before పుస్తకం it expresses the possessor without an English-like apostrophe or an overt additional suffix.

పుస్తకంIN CONTEXT
పుస్తకంbook
book

Combines with the preceding kinship term as 'Grandfather's book'. This whole noun phrase is the identification being denied by కాదు.

కాదుIN CONTEXT
కాదుnegative identification
is not

Rejects the proposed ownership-based identification. The following source sentence uses లేదు for absence, a different claim not merged into this example.

How it fits together

During a search for a family member's book, తాతయ్య precedes పుస్తకం to express the possessor. The sentence denies that the current item is that book. No apostrophe-like marker is necessary in this kinship noun phrase. కాదు negates identification; it does not itself say the grandfather or book is absent.

Notice Do not confuse తాతయ్య with the vocative తాతయ్యా. The full word's grandfather sense is dictionary-supported; its component analysis and possessive use are separate grammatical explanations.

లేదు

lēdu

is not present; does not exist (negative counterpart of uṇḍu)
FREQUENCY 100VERB LABLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Required Verb Lab: ఉండు uṇḍu — be, exist, stay, wait · Negative + affirmative examples below; lēdu is not renamed uṇḍu.

లేదు expresses absent existence or a missing state, using the negative presence stem లే. It connects to required ఉండు without meaning identity-negating కాదు.

Recognize it Link ఉండు → ఉంది/ఉన్నది with negative లే → లేదు; keep కాదు for a different identity/classification contrast.

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction

Restricts the absence claim to this location. The sentence does not assert that the book does not exist anywhere.

పుస్తకంIN CONTEXT

లేదుTARGET

How it fits together

Notice లేదు denies presence; కాదు would deny identification or classification. Neither should be taught as a universally interchangeable English not.

Meaning orderthis.EMPHATIC | my | friend's | house

The final ē focuses the identified house: this is the one. English can convey that identification without explicitly adding the word very.

నాIN CONTEXT

my

Modifies friend rather than house directly. The phrase therefore means the house of my friend, not my own house and a friend.

The final డు of the masculine noun changes to డి in this possessive form. It is stem alternation, not a separately added డి suffix.

ఇల్లుTARGET
ఇల్లుhouse/home

Names the place being identified. Although later case forms can use ఇంటి, this bare predicate noun retains the dictionary form ఇల్లు.

How it fits together

The focused demonstrative stands as subject, followed by a nested possessive noun phrase: my friend, then that friend's house. There is no overt present copula. The noun స్నేహితుడి supplies the possessive relationship through its changed ending; ఇల్లు remains uninflected because the clause simply identifies the house.

Limits the statement to the current time. It does not say the person never visits or has always been absent.

ఇక్కడTARGET

పెద్ద describes size here, modifying a rock. Other contexts can mean older, senior, important, or loud; the noun and situation select the appropriate sense.

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderone/a | big | rock | exists
ఒకIN CONTEXT
ఒకone, indefinite determiner here
a; one

Introduces a single rock. In this existential sentence English a is natural, without forcing a contrast between one rock and two.

పెద్దTARGET
పెద్దlarge in size
big

An adjective placed before the noun it describes. Because the noun is a rock, physical size is intended rather than a person's seniority.

రాయిIN CONTEXT
రాయిrock/stone noun

Names the object whose existence is asserted. This is not the homographic write command; the preceding adjective and following existence verb resolve the sense.

ఉందిIN CONTEXT
ఉందిsingular nonmasculine existence form of ఉండు
there is

Agrees with the singular inanimate rock. English introduces there to form the existential sentence, but Telugu needs no separate equivalent token.

How it fits together

ఒక పెద్ద రాయి forms a noun phrase ordered determiner, adjective, noun. ఉంది then asserts its existence. పెద్ద does not take gender or number agreement here. The construction also disambiguates రాయి: a large stone can exist, whereas reading it as an imperative write would break the syntax.

Notice Do not translate పెద్ద uniformly as elder. A rock calls for big; kinship and personal contexts may select older or senior instead.

పడు has short a and means fall, with contextual extensions such as dust getting into eyes. Its past పడ్డాను must not be mislabeled పడాను.

Recognize it పడు has short a; పాడు has long ā. Past falling పడ్డ- also differs from singing పాడ-.

కళ్ళల్లో దుమ్ము పడుతోంది.

kaḷḷallō dummu paḍutōndi.

Dust is getting into the eyes.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction

కళ్ళల్లోIN CONTEXT

in the eyes

The eye noun has an irregular plural and oblique shape. Keep the source's locative form rather than falsely segmenting it as an unchanged singular.

దుమ్ముIN CONTEXT
దుమ్ముmass-noun subject
dust

Dust is what is falling or getting in. It is the grammatical subject, although the English translation may emphasize the affected eyes.

పడుతోందిTARGET

The contracted progressive ending describes an ongoing event. The initial vowel stays short, preserving the distinction from the singing verb పాడు.

Required Verb Lab: వాసన చూడు vāsana chūḍu — smell (something)

వాసన చూడు is an active smelling expression: scent plus perceive. Keep వాసన present; చూడు alone normally means look, and a thing emitting smell is a different construction.

Recognize it వాసన + చూడు-family form means actively smelling; చూడు alone is not enough to establish that sense.

Meaning orderthis | flower | scent | perceive.IMPERATIVE
ఈIN CONTEXT

this

చూడుTARGET

In this compound, the verb contributes intentional perception rather than literal vision. Only this component would inflect when the sentence changes tense.

How it fits together

The final చూడు is a familiar imperative. The noun వాసన must remain in the expression; otherwise the instruction simply asks the listener to look.

The adverb fixes the proposed outing on today. Retain this attested spelling rather than silently replacing it with ఈరోజు or ఇవాళ.

నాటకంIN CONTEXT
నాటకంstage play/drama
a play

Names the performance to watch. An indefinite article is natural in English, but Telugu has no separate word for a here.

చూద్దాంIN CONTEXT
చూద్దాంfirst-person plural proposal from చూడు
let's watch

This is an invitation to a shared action, not a completed seeing event. The contracted proposal form is best recognized as a whole.

How it fits together

The time expression comes before the object and the proposal verb. చూద్దాం includes the speaker and listener in a suggested action, naturally translated let's watch. No explicit మనం is needed. The time adverb is lexical and should not be analyzed as a tense ending.

Notice Keep ఇవ్వేళ in quotations. A learner may encounter ఇవేళ, ఇవాళ, ఇవ్వాళ or ఈరోజు elsewhere; differences should be noted rather than silently normalized.

five

Specifies the hour on the clock with the following time noun. It remains the ordinary numeral and does not carry a special time suffix.

ఆగు means stop or wait oneself. It is useful when asking someone to pause, rather than describing stopping another object or activity.

8 targets · situation, not synonym list

Sit down for a meal.

Offer food, ask for milk, eat, drink, wash, and describe what you like. Nouns, verbs, and small linking words work together.

“First eat your meal.” → “I want milk.” → “Wait a little.”

010 ↗Food & hospitalityreader #19 · ×40

అయితే

ayitē

then; in that case
FREQUENCY 100LIMITED / VARIANT SUPPORT

At the start of a reply, అయితే connects a choice to what was just said: 'in that case'. Its conditional origin does not make every occurrence 'while'.

Recognize it At the beginning of a reply, అయితే often announces 'in that case…'.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 16 ↗ · Sfc3baf083385

అయితే ఉప్మా కావాలి.

ayitē upmā kāvāli.

In that case, I want upma.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderif-so/then · upma · wanted
అయితేTARGET
అయిbe/become: combining stem+తేconditional
in that case

Historically a conditional of అవు, now linking this reply to previous information. The parts are a grammatical analysis, not an exact returned headword.

ఉప్మాIN CONTEXT
ఉప్మాsavory cooked grain dish
upma

Names the food being chosen. Upma is commonly made with semolina; retaining its food name is clearer than translating it as generic porridge.

కావాలిIN CONTEXT
కావాలిwant/need predicate
is wanted; is needed

Expresses what is wanted without encoding an English-style subject 'I'. The customer or speaker is supplied by the conversational context.

How it fits together

In a food-ordering exchange, అయితే signals a decision based on the preceding information. ఉప్మా is the wanted item and కావాలి states the need or preference. The experiencer can be expressed with a dative form such as నాకు, but is understood here and must not be inserted into the source.

Notice Translate the discourse relation, not a generic list of conjunctions: 'while' is unsupported here. Gwynn explicitly supports 'in that case' under ఐతే; the mapping to the source's expanded అయితే remains variant/grammar analysis.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete ordering reply. Translation supplied; the person wanting the food is understood from the dialogue rather than expressed as a separate Telugu word.

నీకు

nīku

to/for you — one familiar listener; experiencer here
FREQUENCY 100LIMITED / VARIANT SUPPORT

నీకు is the familiar singular dative form. With కావాలి it marks the person wanting something, so English uses subject 'you' even though Telugu uses a dative experiencer.

Recognize it నీకు + wanted item + కావాలా? asks one familiar person what they want.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 16 ↗ · Se50c657bf351

నీకు మినపట్టు కావాలా?

nīku minapaṭṭu kāvālā?

Do you want a black-gram crepe?

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyou-DATIVE · black-gram-crepe · wanted-QUESTION
నీకుTARGET
నీfamiliar singular pronoun stem+కుdative experiencer
to you; you as experiencer

Names the person whose preference is asked about. Unlike plain నీ before a noun, the full dative form relates the listener to the wanting predicate.

మినపట్టుIN CONTEXT
మినపblack gram: combining form+అట్టుcrepe/pancake
black-gram crepe

A fused food name formed from black gram and అట్టు. It names the offered dish; the sentence does not specify every ingredient or recipe detail.

కావాలాIN CONTEXT
కావాలిwant/need predicate+ఆyes/no question
do you want/need?

కావాలి combines with question -ఆ, replacing its final vowel in కావాలా. The dative నీకు, not this ending, identifies the person wanting the food.

How it fits together

At a food choice, నీకు marks the customer or listener as experiencer, మినపట్టు names the offered item, and కావాలా asks whether it is wanted. English restructures this as 'do you want'. The question vowel changes కావాలి to కావాలా; it does not introduce an English-style auxiliary 'do'.

Notice The pronoun is familiar singular, not respectful మీకు. Dative morphology is inferred, and the dish gloss describes its food category rather than guaranteeing a particular recipe.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete first food-choice question, excluding the initial address and following pesarattu question. Translation supplied; మినపట్టు is retained, not silently replaced with a modern menu label.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Crucially, usage 2(i) of కి/కు explicitly illustrates the wanting frame with naaku illu kaawaali. This supports the general dative-experiencer construction, not an exact నీకు analysis: mapping the listener to నీ + కు and the familiar register remains grammar/usage analysis. అన్నం

అన్నం primarily means cooked rice and often extends to food or a meal. In this invitation, meal is more natural English than uncooked rice.

పద, అన్నం తిందాం.

pada, annaṁ tindāṁ.

Come on, let's eat.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning ordercome.on | cooked.rice/meal | eat.LET.US
పదIN CONTEXT
పదfamiliar urging expression
come on

Urges the listener to join or get moving. In this conversational use it is not the noun word and does not carry the meal's meaning.

అన్నంTARGET
అన్నంcooked rice/food
a meal; cooked rice

The object of eating is overt in Telugu. English can leave it implicit in let's eat, but the annotation retains its cooked-food sense.

తిందాంIN CONTEXT
తిందాంfirst-person plural proposal from తిను
let's eat

Includes the speaker in the proposed eating. It is not the same as తిను, which commands only the listener to eat.

How it fits together

An urging expression precedes the object and a first-person plural proposal. తిందాం invites shared eating rather than reporting a past meal. అన్నం is an unmarked inanimate object. The idiomatic English invitation can omit meal, but the Telugu explicitly names food, normally cooked rice or the meal centered on it.

Notice Distinguish అన్నం from అన్న, older brother, and from బియ్యం, uncooked rice. The final anusvara is part of this food word.

నాకు

FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

నాకు is the dative form associated with నేను, using the base నా plus కు. In wants and needs, English often translates the experiencer as I.

Recognize it నా + కు = నాకు; with కావాలి, think to me it is wanted → I want.

I want milk.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderto.me | milk | wanted/needed
నాకుTARGET
నాfirst-person oblique base+కుdative/experiencer
to me; I want

Marks the person experiencing the want. The whole English I want is distributed between this dative pronoun and the final predicate కావాలి.

పాలుIN CONTEXT
పాలుmilk, conventionally plural-form noun
milk

Names the requested drink. Its plural-looking form does not mean several separate milks here; English treats milk as a mass noun.

కావాలిIN CONTEXT
కావాలిwant/need predicate
is wanted; is needed

Completes the dative-experiencer construction. Context makes this a request for milk, while a different situation could favor the stronger English need.

How it fits together

Telugu expresses this want with an experiencer in the dative, followed by the desired thing and కావాలి. English normally recasts the same relationship as I want milk. నాకు is therefore not a nominative I, even though I is the natural translation of the experiencer in the English sentence.

Notice Do not replace నాకు with నేను merely to mirror English I. Use the Telugu predicate's construction, especially with wants, needs, feelings and possession.

ముందు అన్నం తిను.

Eat your meal first.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderfirst | meal | eat.IMPERATIVE
ముందుIN CONTEXT
ముందుpriority in sequence
first

Mundu places eating before the other activity under discussion. Here it means first, not the physical location in front of something.

అన్నంIN CONTEXT
అన్నంcooked rice or meal
meal; cooked rice

Annaṁ is the object eaten and commonly stands for a meal. The noun has no possessive suffix corresponding to English your here.

తినుTARGET
తినుeat: familiar singular imperative
eat!

The dictionary-shaped form functions as a direct command to one familiar person. Its final u belongs to this form, not to a separate tense marker.

How it fits together

Mundu sets the order of activities, annaṁ supplies the object, and tinu gives the instruction. The subject you is omitted because the imperative addresses the listener directly. No tense chart is needed to read this sentence: the bare command requests eating before another activity.

Notice Tinu is not the polite plural command. Annaṁ usually means cooked rice or a meal, not uncooked rice grains.

Tāgu means drink in this coffee sentence. Gwynn also records smoking in a particular cigar expression, which is not the sense being taught here.

మైక్ ఒక కప్పు కాఫీ తాగుతాడు.

maik oka kappu kāphī tāgutāḍu.

Meaning orderMike | one | cup | coffee | drink.NONPAST.HE
మైక్IN CONTEXT
మైక్personal name
Mike

Mike is the subject and the person drinking. The Telugu spelling represents the borrowed personal name without any added case suffix here.

ఒకIN CONTEXT
ఒకone
one; a

Oka counts the cup and naturally translates as a in this context. It is the modifier form, rather than standalone numeral okaṭi.

కప్పుIN CONTEXT
కప్పుcup measure
cup

The cup noun gives the amount of coffee. Its doubled p belongs to the loan noun, not a grammatical suffix or agreement marker.

కాఫీIN CONTEXT
కాఫీcoffee
coffee

Coffee follows the cup measure without an English-style of. Ph represents Telugu ఫ in this transliteration; the loan is commonly pronounced with f.

తాగుతాడుTARGET
తాగుdrink+తాడుnonpast masculine singular
he drinks

Tāgu combines with tāḍu, marking a masculine singular nonpast predicate. The routine context selects drinks rather than a specific future promise to drink.

How it fits together

The subject Mike comes before the quantity phrase oka kappu kāphī and the final verb. Telugu expresses a cup of coffee without a separate of. Tāgutāḍu agrees with the male subject and has a habitual nonpast reading in the description of his routine.

Notice The sentence says drinks, not necessarily is drinking at this instant. Keep the long ā of tāgu.

141 ↗Food & hospitalitysupplement · not a top-100 rank

తినిపించు

tinipinchu

feed someone; give someone food to eat
VERB SUPPLEMENTVERB LABLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Required Verb Lab: తినిపించు tinipinchu — feed (someone)

తినిపించు describes feeding someone, contrasted with తిను, eating oneself. It can express helping a child eat without implying force or a negative situation.

Recognize it తిను is eat; తినిపించు adds a feeding/causative layer, often alongside a recipient marked కు or కి.
NEW EXAMPLE · EDITORIAL

అమ్మ పాపకు అన్నం తినిపించింది.

amma pāpaku annaṁ tinipinchindi.

Mother fed the child rice.

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning ordermother | child.TO | rice | eat.CAUSATIVE.PAST.3SG.NONMASC
అమ్మIN CONTEXT
అమ్మmother noun; subject
mother

Mother is the person providing the food. Her feminine singular reference determines the final agreement form rather than the child receiving it.

పాపకుIN CONTEXT
పాపchild/baby noun+కుdative recipient suffix
to the child

The dative suffix marks the child as recipient of feeding. It distinguishes the eater from the food object అన్నం.

అన్నంIN CONTEXT
అన్నంcooked-rice noun; food object
cooked rice; food

This names what the child is given to eat. The unmarked food object is distinct from the dative-marked recipient.

తినిపించిందిTARGET
తినిbound eating stem related to తిను+పించ్bound causative formation+ిందిpast, third-person singular nonmasculine
she fed

The causative layer changes eating into giving someone food to eat. The final ending agrees with mother, not with the rice.

How it fits together

అమ్మ is the feeder, పాపకు marks the recipient, and అన్నం is the food. The causative verb తినిపించింది makes the distinction between eating and feeding explicit. Its past singular nonmasculine ending agrees with the mother. A causative grammatical structure need not imply coercion; this example describes an ordinary act of care.

Notice Do not use తినిపించు to mean I eat. The dictionary baseline does not automatically validate every Lab causative form.

25 targets · situation, not synonym list

Read, ask, understand.

Read a passage, ask someone to explain it, say something, listen, and understand. Recognition comes first; full conjugation tables are not assigned.

“Listen to this song.” → “Read the novel afterward.” → “Explain this lesson.”

022 ↗Learning & workreader #37 · ×27

కాదు

kādu

is not — denying an identification
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

కాదు denies that something is the named thing or has the asserted identity. This book is not a novel; the sentence does not say the book is absent.

Recognize it Noun description + కాదు often means 'is not that', rather than 'is not there'.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 31 ↗ · S995697021c93

ఇది నవల కాదు.

idi navala kādu.

This is not a novel.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis · novel · is-not
ఇదిIN CONTEXT
ఇదిthing under discussion
this

Points to the book or item whose classification is being corrected. It introduces an existing referent, not a question about which item.

నవలIN CONTEXT
నవలliterary genre
novel

The category that the speaker rejects for this item. The sentence does not provide the correct genre, so no alternative is invented.

కాదుTARGET
కాదుnegative identification
is not

Negates the noun predicate 'a novel'. It does not express absence from a location or simply attach a universal English 'not' to any verb.

How it fits together

The speaker corrects a mistaken classification. ఇది identifies the object, నవల supplies the proposed category, and కాదు rejects that category. Positive nominal identification can omit 'is', but this negative predicate is expressed. Compare absence constructions with లేదు: saying something is not a novel does not say no book is present.

Notice Do not teach కాదు as a universal negative for all tenses, persons, and verbs. Brown's 'It is not (so)' supports the selected negative predicate, not every possible negative construction.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

Exact complete first sentence. The following source sentence is truncated and is excluded. Translation supplied only for the clean identification denial.

Translation and grammar annotation: editorial analysis. The Telugu fragment matches the captured reader text; that match is not a guarantee that the original OCR is correct. No native-speaker review claimed.

Check the definition · sources & limits

Read Brown కాదు (p. 270), explicitly defined as 'It is not (so)' and contrasted with affirmative అవును. This supports the negative predicate in 'This is not a novel'; the entry's additional impossibility senses are not selected. The finer contrast with existential లేదు and restrictions across persons/tenses remain grammar analysis. Gwynn's broader search returned only కాదుకూడదను, which is not substituted for this entry.

ఇష్టం

iṣṭaṁ

liking; fondness
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

ఇష్టం names liking rather than acting like English subject-plus-'like'. In this school scenario, a dative person experiences the liking and అంటే introduces what she likes.

Recognize it Dative person + X అంటే + ఇష్టం: the person likes X.
SOURCE SENTENCEMini Story 4 ↗ · M944a2568215a

కూతురికి స్కూల్ అంటే ఇష్టం

kūturiki skūl aṇṭē iṣṭaṁ

The daughter likes school.

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderdaughter-DATIVE · school · regarding · liking
కూతురికిIN CONTEXT
కూతురిdaughter: oblique stem+కిdative experiencer
to the daughter; the daughter as experiencer

కూతురు changes to కూతురి- before -కి. The dative marks the person who feels the liking, not a destination or the thing liked.

స్కూల్IN CONTEXT
స్కూల్school: liked topic
school

The thing toward which the daughter's liking is directed. This English loanword keeps the exact final consonant spelling used by the public source.

అంటేIN CONTEXT
అంటేintroduces object of feeling
as for; toward, in this feeling frame

Links school to the feeling predicate. Here it does not ask what the word 'school' means, despite అంటే having that function in other constructions.

ఇష్టంTARGET
ఇష్టంpositive feeling
liking; fondness

Names the feeling experienced by the daughter. It functions as the predicate without a separate overt ఉంది in this concise sentence.

How it fits together

The school-liking scenario has three roles: కూతురికి identifies the experiencer, స్కూల్ అంటే identifies the object of feeling, and ఇష్టం names that feeling. English reorganizes these roles into 'the daughter likes school'. Telugu does not require an English-style transitive verb 'like' or an overt copula in this example.

Notice Do not mechanically replace కూతురికి with an unmarked subject when building this pattern. అంటే here is a feeling-frame connector, not a dictionary-definition 'means'.

మన

mana

our — including the listener
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

మన modifies a noun shared by the speaker and listener. Calling a school 'our school' includes the listener in that affiliation, without necessarily claiming legal ownership.

Recognize it మన immediately before a noun means 'our, including you'; మనం can itself be the subject.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 5 ↗ · Sb009d3dffe61

ఇది మన స్కూలు.

idi mana skūlu.

This is our school, yours and mine.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis · our-INCLUSIVE · school
ఇదిIN CONTEXT
ఇదిplace being identified
this

Points to the school or its representation as a whole. It stands independently as the sentence topic, not as a modifier meaning 'which'.

మనTARGET
మనinclusive possessor/affiliation
our, including you

Includes both speaker and listener in the school community. The possessive relation can express attendance or affiliation, not just physical ownership.

స్కూలుIN CONTEXT
స్కూలుschool: Telugu-shaped loan
school

The English loan 'school' appears with a final Telugu vowel. That final -ు is not a plural marker or a separate case suffix here.

How it fits together

This identification links a place to a shared school community. మన directly modifies స్కూలు, while ఇది stands for the place being pointed out. No overt 'is' is required. The crucial contrast is inclusion: మన includes the listener in 'our', whereas మా normally presents the speaker's group without that listener.

Notice మన is possessive 'our'; మనం is subject 'we'. The school's relationship to the speakers need not be legal ownership.

ఇంకాTARGET

some more

Adds further study to what has already been done. The prior attempt explains the continuation reading, without requiring a negative verb in this sentence.

చదవాలిIN CONTEXT

your

Marks the name as belonging to the person addressed. It pairs naturally with a familiar singular command, rather than a respectful plural form.

పేరుIN CONTEXT

name

Names what the person lacks. The word is grammatically singular nonmasculine, so the negative existence predicate is లేదు rather than masculine లేడు.

నాలుగు

నాలుగు is the ordinary numeral four. In this counting sentence the number is exact; idiomatic uses meaning a few or several are not intended.

Recognize it నాలుగు = four things in this pattern; recognize నలుగురు separately for four people.

Refers independently to a set of objects away from the speaker or already identified. Its plural reference matches the four pens.

నాలుగుTARGET
నాలుగుcardinal numeral four
four

Provides the exact count in this exercise. The final గు belongs to the numeral and is not an object-count classifier to separate.

కలాలుIN CONTEXT

The plural of కలం has the surface form కలాలు. The anusvara is lost and the preceding vowel lengthens; this is not a simple unchanged concatenation.

How it fits together

The demonstrative subject is followed by a numeral and plural noun as predicate, with present are left unexpressed. నాలుగు gives an exact quantity here. The noun కలం changes to కలాలు in the plural, so its analysis must account for the stem change rather than merely appending లు to the singular spelling.

Notice For four people, నలుగురు is the usual dedicated human-count form. Do not apply the object-count example mechanically to a group of people.

పుస్తకాలు

pustakālu

books
FREQUENCY 100LEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

This is the plural of pustakaṁ, book, not a separate kind of object. The plural replaces the singular ending and has a long ā.

Recognize it The sequence pustak- stays recognizable; pustakaṁ is one book and pustakālu is books.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 4 ↗ · S161ff1ad927c

ఈ పుస్తకాలు ఎన్ని?

How many books are these?

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthese | books | how.many
ఈIN CONTEXT
ఈnear demonstrative
these

This noun modifier points to nearby books. Its shape stays ī before both singular and plural nouns in Telugu.

పుస్తకాలుTARGET
పుస్తకాplural stem of pustakaṁ+లుplural
books

Pustakaṁ becomes pustakālu, with the singular aṁ replaced by ālu. The displayed stem includes that vowel change rather than preserving aṁ.

ఎన్నిIN CONTEXT
ఎన్నిnonhuman count question
how many

Enni asks for the number of countable things here. It supplies the question itself, so no additional yes/no ā is needed.

How it fits together

Ī modifies the plural noun without changing its own form. Enni asks for a count of books rather than people or an uncountable amount. This is a present nominal question: English supplies are, while Telugu simply places the question word after the noun phrase.

Notice Do not mechanically append lu to the entire singular pustakaṁ; learn the actual plural pustakālu.

కథ

SOURCE SENTENCEMini Story 8 ↗ · Me35d761ad098

ఇదే కథ మరో విధంగా చెప్పబడింది.

idē katha marō vidhaṅgā cheppabaḍindi.

This same story was told in another way.

Take this sentence apart 5 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis.very | story | another | manner.ADVERB | tell.PASSIVE.PAST
ఇదేIN CONTEXT
ఇదేidi with emphatic ē
this very; the same

Idi, this, combines with emphatic ē and surfaces as idē. Here it identifies the same story, not a newly introduced different story.

కథTARGET
కథstory
story

This singular noun is what gets told. The passive construction makes the story the grammatical subject rather than the teller.

మరోIN CONTEXT
మరోanother
another

Marō modifies the following manner expression. It contrasts how the story is presented, without saying that the story itself is different.

విధంగాIN CONTEXT
విధంmanner+గాadverbial
in a way

Vidhaṁ, manner, takes gā to function adverbially. The written anusvara is pronounced with the following g, giving the transliteration vidhaṅgā.

చెప్పబడిందిIN CONTEXT

was told

The ceppa stem is followed by passive baḍu in its past singular form baḍindi. The construction reports telling without naming who told it.

How it fits together

Idē katha is the subject: this very story. Marō vidhaṅgā supplies an adverbial phrase describing the manner of telling. Ceppabaḍindi is passive and past, so English uses was told. The teller is omitted; the sentence focuses on the story and the alternative presentation.

Notice Ceppabaḍindi is a passive form, not simply she said. The story is the thing told.

నేర్పించు

nērpinchu

teach
VERB SUPPLEMENTVERB LABLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Required Verb Lab: నేర్పించు nērpinchu — teach

Nērpiñcu means teach, with the learner receiving instruction. It must not be confused with nērcukō, learn; nērpu is a related teaching verb, not this exact surface.

Recognize it Nērpiñcu is teach; connect nērpis- in its nonpast family with teaching, not nērcukō learning.
NEW EXAMPLE · EDITORIAL

నాకు తెలుగు నేర్పించు.

Teach me Telugu.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderme.TO | Telugu | teach.IMPERATIVE
నాకుIN CONTEXT

తెలుగుIN CONTEXT
తెలుగుlanguage taught
Telugu

The language name identifies the content of instruction. It has no added suffix and stands between the learner and the teaching command.

నేర్పించుTARGET

The verified teaching headword also serves as a familiar command here. Keep the full nērpiñcu form; do not substitute the learning verb nērcukō.

How it fits together

Nāku identifies the learner, telugu identifies what is taught, and nērpiñcu addresses the person expected to teach. The subject you is understood from the imperative. This separates teaching from learning: the speaker receives instruction while the addressee supplies it. Only this command and the base meaning are illustrated.

నేర్చుకో

nērchukō

learn
VERB SUPPLEMENTVERB LABLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Required Verb Lab: నేర్చుకో nērchukō — learn

Nērcukō is the familiar command learn, from nērcukonu. It describes acquiring a skill, whereas nērpu and nērpiñcu describe teaching someone else a skill.

Notice Preserve gūḍā rather than silently changing it to kūḍā. Nērcukō is not nērpu or nērpiñcu, teach.

చదువుకో

chaduvukō

Caduvukō belongs to caduvukonu, study or read to oneself. The snapshot supplies a genuine studying participle even though the batch's automatic verb matches are empty.

Recognize it Recognize caduvukuṇṭū through caduvukonu/caduvukō. Its final tū marks a linked studying activity, not a stand-alone I study sentence.

అతను ఇంటి పట్టునే ఉంటూ, చదువుకుంటూ, డబ్బు ఆదా చేయాలని నిర్ణయించుకున్నాడు

He decided to stay at home, study, and save money.

Meaning orderhe | house.OBLIQUE | at.home.EMPHATIC | staying | studying | money | saving | do.SHOULD.QUOTE | decide.PAST.HE
అతనుIN CONTEXT
అతనుmasculine subject
he

Atanu is the person making the decision and carrying out the planned activities. The final finite verb agrees with this male singular referent.

ఇంటిIN CONTEXT
ఇంటిoblique stem of illu

Illu changes to iṇṭi in this established location expression. Iṇṭi paṭṭuna refers to staying at home, not holding a physical house.

పట్టునేIN CONTEXT
పట్టునేpaṭṭuna with emphatic ē

The fixed expression iṇṭi paṭṭuna means at home. Emphatic ē changes the ending to paṭṭunē, highlighting remaining at home rather than going away.

ఉంటూIN CONTEXT
ఉంటూuṇḍu: continuing participle

Uṇḍu appears as the participial uṇṭū, staying or while being. The long tū ending links an ongoing circumstance to the rest of the planned activity.

The caduvukonu family uses caduvukuṇṭū for studying as a linked activity. The konu component belongs to study for oneself; final tū is participial, not person agreement.

డబ్బుIN CONTEXT
డబ్బుmoney
money

Ḍabbu names what will be saved. It is a mass noun here with no plural or overt object ending, despite English referring to an amount.

ఆదాIN CONTEXT
ఆదాsaving

Ādā combines with cēyu, do, to mean save. The two-word predicate ādā cēyu is about conserving money, not physically rescuing a person.

చేయాలనిIN CONTEXT
చేయాలిshould do: underlying form+అనిcontent or quotation linker

Underlying cēyāli plus ani contracts to cēyālani. In a decision construction it expresses intended action, here saving money, rather than an externally imposed obligation.

నిర్ణయించుకున్నాడుIN CONTEXT

he decided

Nirṇayiñcu joins the konu-family past component kunnāḍu to express his own decision. Masculine singular agreement identifies he, and the completed form gives decided.

How it fits together

The final past verb reports a decision. The intended content includes staying at home, studying, and saving money: uṇṭū and caduvukuṇṭū are linking participles, not separate finite sentences. Ādā cēyālani combines save with the content linker ani, while the subject atanu is shared across the planned activities.

Listen to this song.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis | song | listen.IMPERATIVE
ఈIN CONTEXT
ఈnear or current demonstrative
this

Ī selects the particular song being presented. The demonstrative modifies the noun without taking a case ending or an independent subject role here.

పాటIN CONTEXT
పాటsong
song

Pāṭa is the object of listening. Telugu leaves it unmarked here even though the English verb listen normally requires the preposition to.

వినుTARGET
వినుlisten: familiar singular imperative
