Vinu is a direct command to attend to the song. Context chooses deliberate listening from the broader hear/listen meanings, not the unrelated classical noun sky.

How it fits together

Ī pāṭa forms the object phrase this song, followed by the listening imperative vinu. The addressed subject you is understood. English supplies to after listen, but the Telugu sentence needs no corresponding separate token. This command requests deliberate attention rather than reporting that a sound was accidentally heard.

Notice Vinu is familiar singular. Pāṭa, song, differs from pāṭhaṁ, lesson; do not add an aspiration absent from the source.

వివరించు means explain or describe with detail. It suits a lesson or explanation request and is more specific than simply saying or telling something.

Required Verb Lab: ఆజ్ఞాపించు ājñāpinchu — command, order

ఆజ్ఞాపించు is formal and authority-laden: command or order. It is not the neutral everyday choice for telling a friend to do something.

Recognize it ఆజ్ఞ means an order; ఆజ్ఞాపించు reports commanding. Notice the reported instruction before the main verb.

The officer ordered someone to sit down.

అధికారిIN CONTEXT
అధికారిofficial noun; subject
officer; official

The noun itself does not force a male interpretation. The masculine singular ending of the example's verb supplies that reference.

కూర్చోమనిIN CONTEXT
కూర్చోsit-down imperative base+మనిreported-command linker including అని
to sit down; saying sit

This packages the instruction sit down as the content of the order. మని is kept together rather than mislabeled as tense or person.

The past form reports an authoritative order. It is not a polite request simply because the vocabulary is formal or Sanskrit-derived.

How it fits together

The official is the subject, and కూర్చోమని presents the reported instruction that was issued. The recipient is not expressed, so English someone is explanatory rather than a translated token. ఆజ్ఞాపించాడు gives a past event with masculine singular agreement. The formal commanding verb conveys authority; neutral everyday telling is usually better illustrated with చెప్పు.

Notice Formal does not mean polite. Avoid using this as a casual request formula with friends, family, or service staff.

అర్థం చేసుకో

arthaṁ chēsukō

understand; make sense of
VERB SUPPLEMENTVERB LABLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Required Verb Lab: అర్థం చేసుకో arthaṁ chēsukō — understand

అర్థం చేసుకో combines meaning with doing for oneself to express understanding. Preserve both components; అర్థం కాలేదు is a useful but grammatically different not-understood construction.

Recognize it Keep అర్థం beside చేసుకో/చేసుకున్న-; distinguish that family from అర్థం అవుతుంది or అర్థం కాలేదు.
NEW EXAMPLE · EDITORIAL

ఈ వాక్యాన్ని అర్థం చేసుకో.

ī vākyānni arthaṁ chēsukō.

Make sense of this sentence.

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderthis | sentence.OBJECT | meaning | do.SELF-BENEFIT.IMPERATIVE
ఈIN CONTEXT
ఈproximal demonstrative
this

This points to the sentence under discussion. The following noun, not the demonstrative, carries the marking identifying what should be understood.

వాక్యాన్నిIN CONTEXT
వాక్యాbound accusative stem of వాక్యం+న్నిaccusative surface ending for this noun class
the sentence; as object

The noun changes shape in the accusative. This surface-oriented division records the long vowel and doubled న్న rather than appending ని to unchanged వాక్యం.

అర్థంTARGET
అర్థంmeaning noun; required compound component
meaning; sense

This noun is essential to the understand expression. It remains independent in spelling and does not itself carry the imperative or tense.

చేసుకోTARGET
చేసుbound combining stem related to చేయు+కోకొను auxiliary; familiar imperative
make for oneself; understand with అర్థం

The self-oriented auxiliary joins the doing stem. Within the complete expression, the natural meaning is understand, not perform a separate action for oneself.

How it fits together

ఈ వాక్యాన్ని is the object to be understood. The expression అర్థం చేసుకో combines the noun meaning with a self-oriented చేయు–కొను verb form, and the final కో gives a familiar imperative. This differs from అర్థం కాలేదు, where understanding is described as not having occurred. The latter cannot substitute as an attested inflection of చేసుకో.

Notice The complete అర్థం చేసుకొను compound is dictionary-supported, but the example is generated/editorial. Do not substitute a noun-only match or an అవు-family sentence as proof of this command or a full paradigm.

Dictionary support does not make the sentence corpus-attested or native-reviewed.

13 targets · situation, not synonym list

Get somewhere and do things.

Come, go, stand, walk, drive, wait, and handle objects. The rarer required verbs belong here as optional extensions, not mandatory day-one material.

“Come too.” → “Go to your home.” → “Wait a little.”

గూడా

gūḍā

too; also — source variant of కూడా
FREQUENCY 100LIMITED / VARIANT SUPPORT

గూడా adds another participant in this invitation. The corpus uses this variant where modern learning materials commonly use కూడా; preserve it for recognition without inventing a separate meaning.

Recognize it గూడా after a person or thing usually adds it: 'that one too'. Compare కూడా.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 27 ↗ · Sfdeb883f5aaa

నువ్వు గూడా రా!

nuvvu gūḍā rā!

You come too!

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderyou-FAMILIAR · also · come!
నువ్వుIN CONTEXT
నువ్వుfamiliar singular listener
you

Names the additional person being invited on the outing. It matches the direct familiar command at the end of the sentence.

గూడాTARGET
గూడాaddition; variant of కూడా
too

Adds the listener to the existing cinema group. This spelling is retained from the source, not silently replaced by the more familiar కూడా.

రాIN CONTEXT
రాcome: familiar imperative
come!

An irregular familiar command meaning 'come'. It is a complete verb form, not a detachable suffix added to the preceding word.

How it fits together

The speaker has already mentioned going to a film and now includes another listener. The additive particle follows that listener's pronoun, so the meaning is 'you too', not 'come again'. రా is the familiar singular imperative of the verb 'come', a form worth recognizing whole.

Notice Do not translate గూడా as a separate action or attach it to రా. The exact variant remains only partially dictionary-verified.

ఫ్రాన్స్ కి వెళ్లాలని అనుకుంటున్నాను

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderFrance · to · go-SHOULD-QUOTATIVE · thinking-FIRST-SINGULAR
ఫ్రాన్స్IN CONTEXT
ఫ్రాన్స్country name: destination

The destination of the intended journey. This is a proper country name, and the following separately written case marker belongs grammatically with it.

కిTARGET
కిdative destination
to

Marks France as the destination here. Source spacing is preserved, although this marker normally attaches to its noun in continuous Telugu writing.

A progressive first-person form of అనుకొను expressing intention here. The lemma changes stem in conjugation; the displayed parts are explanatory, not a literal concatenation.

How it fits together

A travel intention combines destination ఫ్రాన్స్ కి with a quoted thought: వెళ్లాలి plus అని becomes వెళ్లాలని. అనుకుంటున్నాను presents that thought as the speaker's current intention. The dative marks the destination, while the verb ending supplies 'I'. The separate source token కి should be recognized as a suffix without changing the quotation.

Notice కి is not infinitival English 'to' before a verb, and the returned కీ 'key' is an unrelated word. Other dative uses, such as experiencers, need their own context.

నువ్వు గూడా రా!

Meaning orderyou.FAMILIAR | also | come.IMPERATIVE.SINGULAR
నువ్వుIN CONTEXT
నువ్వుfamiliar singular second person

Explicitly adds this listener to the invitation. Telugu could omit the pronoun in a command, but here it helps contrast the additional invitee.

గూడాIN CONTEXT
గూడాadditive particle, variant of కూడా
also; too

The voiced initial గ reflects the source variant of కూడా. It marks addition to the group, not a different tense or a locative ending.

రాTARGET

This is the main motion command, not a familiar address clitic appended to another instruction. Its long ā is essential to the written form.

How it fits together

Notice రా is informal singular; రండి is respectful or plural. Here it is not the address particle rā or a substring inside a person's name.

Following an activity noun, లేదు denies that activity's occurrence. The understood male subject comes from context, not from this word's ending.

How it fits together

The activity noun నడవటం combines with లేదు to say that walking is not happening. ఇంకా adds the not-yet meaning, appropriate to the child discussed in the preceding turn. This construction does not use a regular positive finite conjugation. The source spelling టం is retained rather than normalized to డం.

Notice Do not equate the Lab citation నడవు with an independently verified canonical dictionary headword or teach its proposed hortative as established.

some

Indicates several unspecified arrangements without giving an exact number. It does not necessarily imply that the quantity is unusually small or inadequate.

ఏర్పాట్లుIN CONTEXT
ఏర్పాటుarrangement, lemma+లుplural with stem-vowel loss
arrangements

The plural of ఏర్పాటు loses the final ఉ before లు in this spelling. Note the actual ఏర్పాట్లు form rather than inventing an unchanged concatenation.

చెయ్యాలిIN CONTEXT
చెయ్యాలిnecessity form of చెయ్యి/చేయు
must do; need to make

Expresses a requirement rather than a completed action. With arrangements, English naturally uses make; the Telugu verb is the general do verb.

How it fits together

A purpose phrase precedes the indefinite plural object and a necessity predicate. కొన్ని selects plural countable arrangements. The Telugu clause leaves the responsible person unexpressed; English can use a passive paraphrase without claiming that the Telugu verb itself is morphologically passive. చెయ్యాలి expresses what needs doing for the proposed event.

Notice For some people, కొందరు is normally the relevant form; కొన్ని belongs with things such as arrangements, books or tickets.
Sentence source & editorial decisions

వాడు

vāḍu

he/that man (pronoun); use! (separate verb homograph)
FREQUENCY 100VERB LABLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Required Verb Lab: వాడు vāḍu — use · Two different senses below. Pronoun frequency is not verb-use frequency.

Keep two independently reviewed senses: వాడు as a familiar male pronoun, and వాడు as the use verb. Neither is derived from the other or from wither.

Recognize it Two senses, one spelling: వాడు + location + ఉన్నాడు = he; object + వాడు! = use! Keep the Verb Lab use sense separate.
SOURCE SENTENCEIntensive Lesson 7 ↗ · S8a3e52b19d4f

వాడు అమెరికాలో ఉన్నాడు.

He is in America.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderhe.FAMILIAR | America.IN | be.MASCULINE.SINGULAR
వాడుTARGET
వాడుfamiliar third-person masculine pronoun
he; that man

Occupies the subject position before a location and a finite be verb. This establishes the pronoun sense independently of the identical use imperative.

అమెరికాలోIN CONTEXT
అమెరికాAmerica, place name+లోin/location
in America

The locative suffix attaches to the place name. It specifies where he is, not where he is traveling or what object he uses.

ఉన్నాడుIN CONTEXT
ఉన్నాడుmasculine singular presence form of ఉండు
he is; he is staying

Agrees with the male pronoun and locates him in America. It need not imply permanent residence, which would require more contextual evidence.

How it fits together

The pronoun subject precedes a locative phrase and a masculine singular existence predicate. This independently identifies వాడు as he, because ఉన్నాడు supplies the clause's verb. America is introduced with లో for location. The sentence says where the man is, without asserting a particular duration of residence.

Notice Pronoun వాడు can sound overly familiar or disrespectful for some adults. It does not teach the required use verb merely by sharing the spelling.

పని denotes work or a task. Here it means something the speaker has to do now, not necessarily paid employment or a permanent job.

ఇప్పుడు నాకు పని ఉంది.

I have work to do now.

Take this sentence apart 4 words · meaning + construction
Meaning ordernow | to.me | work | exists
ఇప్పుడుIN CONTEXT
ఇప్పుడుcurrent time
now

Frames the speaker's immediate availability. It does not imply that the task is habitual or that the speaker has a new permanent job.

నాకుIN CONTEXT
నాfirst-person oblique base+కుdative possessor/concerned person
to me; I have

Identifies whose work or obligation is at issue. In the English have construction the dative experiencer becomes the grammatical subject I.

పనిTARGET
పనిwork/task
work; something to do

Names the activity or obligation occupying the speaker. Nothing in this short sentence forces an employment sense rather than an ordinary task.

ఉందిIN CONTEXT
ఉందిsingular nonmasculine existence form of ఉండు
there is; exists

Combines with the dative pronoun to express having work. It does not itself mean doing; English to do makes the situation idiomatic.

How it fits together

The time adverb precedes a dative possession construction: to me work exists. Telugu has no separate have verb here. పని determines singular nonmasculine agreement on ఉంది. Notice పని can be a task or business, not only a job. Distinguish this from the narrower employment noun ఉద్యోగం when teaching contextual meaning.

నిలబడు

nilabaḍu

stand; stand up; remain standing
VERB SUPPLEMENTVERB LABLEXICAL SENSE CHECKED

Required Verb Lab: నిలబడు nilabaḍu — stand, stand up

నిలబడు describes standing or remaining in place. It differs from sitting down, and its extended dictionary senses include staying, waiting, and enduring.

Recognize it Connect నిలబడు with standing; the related past stem నిలబడ్డ- has doubled ḍ, unlike singing పాడ-.
NEW EXAMPLE · EDITORIAL

ఇక్కడ కాసేపు నిలబడు.

ikkaḍa kāsēpu nilabaḍu.

Stand here for a while.

Take this sentence apart 3 words · meaning + construction
Meaning orderhere | a-while | stand.IMPERATIVE
ఇక్కడIN CONTEXT
ఇక్కడproximal place reference
here

This points to a place near the speaker. It answers where to stand without adding a separate locative ending.

కాసేపుIN CONTEXT
కాసేపుwhole short-duration expression
for a while

This compact duration expression tells how long the person should stand. It is kept whole rather than assigned an artificial case boundary.

నిలబడుTARGET
నిలబడుwhole familiar imperative
stand!

The complete lexical verb functions as a familiar instruction here. Its internal history need not be treated as productive suffixation.

How it fits together

ఇక్కడ sets the location and కాసేపు sets the duration before the sentence-final command. The listener is understood, so a pronoun is unnecessary. నిలబడు is an intransitive instruction: no object is being stood somewhere. This example teaches the bare command, not an unverified set of past and future forms.

Notice Related నిల్చుండు expressions should not be silently presented as mechanically generated forms of నిలబడు.

చూడ- plus ండి yields చూడండి. The respectful command draws the listener's attention before the statement describing the running boy.

ఎవరోIN CONTEXT

Adding long ō to the who base makes an indefinite expression. Here it modifies the boy as someone not identified.

This masculine singular subject controls the corresponding final verb ending. Its డు is part of the noun, not past tense.

అటుIN CONTEXT
అటుdistal direction reference
that way

This indicates a direction away from the speaker's here. It describes where the running is directed, rather than the destination's name.

How it fits together

The polite attention-getter చూడండి precedes a separate statement. ఎవరో కుర్రాడు supplies an unidentified masculine subject, అటు gives direction, and పరిగెత్తుతున్నాడు places running in progress. The indefinite ō in ఎవరో is not a yes/no question ending. Preserve the source's vowel variant instead of silently rewriting it.

బంతినిIN CONTEXT

How it fits together

A spelling match is not sense verification: చేయి can be “hand” or “do!”; రాయి can be “stone” or “write!”; వాడు can be a pronoun or a verb. The selected example—not its count alone—establishes the intended use.

Audit trail · read once, then study

How the set was selected.
What “checked” actually means.

The 100-word selection rule

Reproduced the reader’s displayed Frequency order on 10 September 2026, not a general Telugu corpus ranking. Kept the first 100 accepted surface entries, ending at reader rank 157, after explicitly excluding names/speaker labels, transparent English loans, and selected redundant variants. Ties retain the reader’s dataset order.

Not every historical loan is excluded: integrated useful words such as pustakaṁ or iṣṭaṁ remain useful Telugu. Some inflected forms and similar expressions remain because this is a faithful surface-entry-based selection, not a perfect lemma curriculum. The exclusions are editorial and listed below.

Reader-count limitation: the current reader merges duplicate GUID rows by keeping the first lexical entry (Mini Stories before Intensive) and displays that entry’s n, rather than summing both corpora. Labels for dialogue speakers inflate names. Counts on this page reproduce that behavior; they are neither aggregate counts nor counts of a particular sense. In particular the frequency of వాడు does not establish how often the verb “use” occurs.

Priority supplements ఎక్కడ, ఎప్పుడు, ఎలా, ఎంత are outside the filtered first 100. All 54 verb IDs are covered through eight existing target records and 46 additional records. 150 records does not mean 150 unrelated roots.

Definition, source sentence, and grammar are different checks

Lexical sense checked: an actually retrieved dictionary entry supports the stated headword/sense, sometimes under a combined entry or alternate headword. Homonyms were inspected instead of accepting the first search result. A separate clock-hour entry was needed for గంట; the first exact result concerned stubble.

Limited/variant support: the base is documented but a contraction, possessive mapping, pragmatic use, or exact variant was not directly attested in the returned source. These cards retain a visible limitation. An entry not found is not proof a form is wrong.

Source sentence: exact substring checked against the extracted public reader data. English and grammar were analyzed separately; a full-turn English translation was not blindly applied to a short fragment. Existing OCR can still contain errors. Suspect fragments were avoided or adapted with disclosed changes.

New/adapted example: clearly marked, never presented as a human-translated corpus line. A dictionary-supported verb does not certify the naturalness of a generated sentence. These examples are candidates for a small native-speaker review before high-repetition production practice.

The Verb Lab’s complete conjugation tables were not used as a source of truth and have not been validated by this work. This project verifies selected meanings and analyzes selected forms, not every possible paradigm.

Why these “islands” are situations, not piles of similar words

Language islands generally refer to familiar topics, prepared messages, or areas of conversation a learner can handle. There is no universal clustering metric. Here the eight groups are a navigation aid toward such familiar situations, mixing useful nouns, verbs, pronouns, and markers.

“Similar words together always stick better” is not a safe research claim. Semantically similar new words can interfere. Tinkham (1997) distinguishes semantic from thematic grouping; its findings are a reason for caution, not proof that these particular eight groups are optimal. See the bibliographic summary.

The question compass is deliberately a contrast reference. If where/when/how are all new, introduce one or two in scenes rather than drilling the entire chart cold. If you already know them but confuse them, compare two and attend to the expected answer type.

Study 3–5 items from material you actually read. Recognition of a verb’s root alone will not decode every inflection; the “Recognize it” note bridges the base to the chosen surface without assigning a conjugation table.

Sources, rights, and portability
  • Reader frequency list and its public Mini Stories / Intensive data; 8 Mini Stories and 64 Intensive lessons in this snapshot.
  • Verb Lab: 54 requested entries. Corrected aliases are shown rather than silently changing the inventory.
  • Gwynn, A Telugu-English Dictionary (Oxford University Press, 1991), hosted by DSAL. Per-target evidence links identify actual checks. Dictionary text is not bundled into this page as a replacement database.
  • Teach Me Telugu: source-course context. This page’s corpus check used the user’s reader snapshot, not a claim that every OCR line was rechecked against the printed book or audio.

Sentence excerpts and their source works retain their respective rights. This local companion is not a grant to republish the source course or LingQ collection. No audio files are mirrored. The separate raw snapshot and lookup audit are local working evidence, not intended as a public course download.

This page works offline, including filters and internal navigation. External source links require internet. The TSV has plain-text fields with contextual prompts; it is optional and is not an invitation to add 150 new cards at once. Source example, origin, evidence status and family notes travel with the exports.
